# log1p-xeon8573c-2026-11-21.txt (run 44885832313, main at a1db6df), but bench/record.py refuses to
# join it to that file, since the code under Log1pBenchmark differs between a1db6df and b283273 in
# more than comments (below), so it is a first run of new code. Run 2 below is its re-run, which
# issue #30's rules wait for, dispatched the same way on main at ff185a6, for
# (Expm1|Log1p|Log|Log10)Benchmark: run 45125529045, 23 November 2026, from 06:39 UTC. This file
# holds Log1pBenchmark's blocks and rows of both runs. bench/record.py write made it from the two

# header; "Array" describes the benchmark; each min/avg/max table is taken from its run's JMH
# "Result" blocks in the raw output.
#
# The code: between a1db6df and b283273, the files under Log1pBenchmark that bench/record.py finds
# different in more than comments are Log1p.java and pom.xml. Log1p.java took #100 (5a452fa), the
# allocation-free SPECIES_MAX finish, so the finishMax rows of both runs time the finish that writes
# out its first eight lanes with constant indices, where the 21 November file's boxed on mixed data.
# pom.xml moved JUnit, Surefire and other plugin versions and added repositories. The other commits
# that touched Log1p.java changed comments only. The JMH options include -foe true, so a row that
# failed would have failed the run; every row of Log1pBenchmark has a Result block, which
# bench/record.py requires.
#

NOTE: Current JVM experimentally supports Compiler Blackholes, and they are in use. Please exercise
extra caution when trusting the results, look into the generated code to check the benchmark still
works, and factor in a small probability of new VM bugs. Additionally, while comparisons between
different JVMs are already problematic, the performance difference caused by different Blackhole
modes can be very significant. Please make sure you use the consistent Blackhole mode for comparisons.
